[DEBUG] [Q1] Code generated by claude:
# Get transactions from the last 8 weeks excluding the current week
# Current week starts from the most recent Monday
# We need transactions from 8 weeks before that
transactions_info = Transactions.WHERE(
    # Filter for transactions in the last 9 weeks (we'll exclude current week later)
    DATEDIFF("weeks", date_time, DATETIME("now")) <= 8
).CALCULATE(
    # Calculate the week number relative to now
    weeks_ago = DATEDIFF("weeks", date_time, DATETIME("now")),
    # Get the day of week (1=Monday, 7=Sunday based on the start_of_week config)
    day_of_week = DAYOFWEEK(date_time),
    # Check if it's a weekend (Saturday=6, Sunday=7)
    is_weekend = (DAYOFWEEK(date_time) == 6) | (DAYOFWEEK(date_time) == 7)
)

# Filter out the current week (weeks_ago = 0) and group by week
result = transactions_info.WHERE(
    weeks_ago > 0
).PARTITION(
    name="weekly_groups", 
    by=weeks_ago
).CALCULATE(
    weeks_ago = weeks_ago,
    total_transactions = COUNT(Transactions),
    weekend_transactions = SUM(Transactions.CALCULATE(is_weekend_int = IFF(is_weekend, 1, 0)).is_weekend_int)
).ORDER_BY(weeks_ago.ASC())

SELECT
  _s0.weeks_ago,
  COALESCE(_s0.agg_0, 0) AS total_transactions,
  COALESCE(_s1.agg_1, 0) AS weekend_transactions
FROM _s0 AS _s0
LEFT JOIN _s1 AS _s1
  ON _s0.weeks_ago = _s1.weeks_ago
ORDER BY
  weeks_ago

[DEBUG] [Q1] Code generated by gemini:
# Define the time boundaries for the last 8 weeks, excluding the current week.
# 'start of week' truncates the current timestamp to the preceding Monday.
start_of_the_current_week = DATETIME('now', 'start of week')
# We then subtract 8 weeks to define the beginning of our analysis period.
start_of_the_lookback_period = DATETIME(start_of_the_current_week, '-8 weeks')

# 1. Filter transactions to our specific criteria:
#    - Must be for tickers of type 'stock'.
#    - Must fall within our defined 8-week time window.
# 2. For each of these transactions, calculate the start date of the week it belongs to.
#    This will be our key for grouping.
transactions_in_period = Transactions.WHERE(
    (LOWER(ticker.ticker_type) == 'stock') &
    (date_time >= start_of_the_lookback_period) &
    (date_time < start_of_the_current_week)
).CALCULATE(
    week_start=DATETIME(date_time, 'start of week')
)

# 3. Partition (group) the filtered transactions by their calculated week_start date.
# 4. For each weekly group, calculate the total number of transactions and the count of weekend transactions.
#    Weekend days are identified as 'Saturday' or 'Sunday'.
# 5. Order the results by week in descending order to show the most recent weeks first.
weekly_stock_transactions = transactions_in_period.PARTITION(name='weeks', by=week_start).CALCULATE(
    week_start_date=week_start,
    total_transactions=COUNT(Transactions),
    weekend_transactions=COUNT(Transactions.WHERE(ISIN(DAYNAME(date_time), ('Saturday', 'Sunday'))))
).ORDER_BY(week_start_date.DESC())

[DEBUG] [Q1] Code generated by claude:
# Get the start of the current week (Monday)
current_week_start = Broker.CALCULATE(
    current_week_start=DATETIME('now', 'start of week')
)

# Get transactions for stocks in the last 8 weeks (excluding current week)
stock_transactions = current_week_start.Transactions.WHERE(
    (LOWER(ticker.ticker_type) == LOWER('stock')) &
    (date_time < current_week_start) &
    (date_time >= DATETIME(current_week_start, '-8 weeks'))
).CALCULATE(
    week_start=DATETIME(date_time, 'start of week'),
    day_of_week=DAYOFWEEK(date_time),
    is_weekend=IFF((day_of_week == 6) | (day_of_week == 7), 1, 0)
)

# Group by week and calculate counts
result = stock_transactions.PARTITION(name="weeks", by=week_start).CALCULATE(
    week_start=week_start,
    total_transactions=COUNT(Transactions),
    weekend_transactions=SUM(Transactions.is_weekend)
).ORDER_BY(week_start.DESC())

[DEBUG] [Q1] Code generated by gemini:
# Define the start of the current week and the start of the 8-week period.
# The week is configured to start on Monday.
start_of_this_week = DATETIME('now', 'start of week')
start_of_period = DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-8 weeks')

# Filter transactions to include only stocks within the last 8 full weeks.
recent_stock_transactions = Transactions.WHERE(
    (LOWER(ticker.ticker_type) == 'stock') &
    (date_time >= start_of_period) &
    (date_time < start_of_this_week)
)

# For each transaction, calculate its week's start date and check if it occurred on a weekend.
transactions_with_week_info = recent_stock_transactions.CALCULATE(
    week_start_date=DATETIME(date_time, 'start of week'),
    is_weekend=ISIN(DAYNAME(date_time), ("Saturday", "Sunday"))
)

# Group transactions by their week's start date and calculate the required counts.
weekly_transaction_counts = transactions_with_week_info.PARTITION(
    name="weekly_groups",
    by=(week_start_date)
).CALCULATE(
    week_start_date=week_start_date,
    total_transactions=COUNT(Transactions),
    weekend_transactions=SUM(Transactions.is_weekend)
).ORDER_BY(week_start_date.ASC())

result = weekly_transaction_counts

            date_time,
            '-' || CAST(CAST(STRFTIME('%w', DATETIME(date_time)) AS INTEGER) AS TEXT) || ' days',
            'start of day'

        )

        CAST(STRFTIME('%w', date_time) AS INTEGER) = 6
        OR CAST(STRFTIME('%w', date_time) AS INTEGER) = 7,
        1,

            'start of day'

          'start of day'
        )
      )
    ) AS INTEGER) AS REAL) / 7 AS INTEGER) AS weeks_ago
  FROM _t4
  GROUP BY
    CAST(CAST(CAST((
      JULIANDAY(
        DATE(

            'start of day'
          ),
          'start of day'
        )
      )
    ) AS INTEGER) AS REAL) / 7 AS INTEGER)
)
SELECT
  _s0.weeks_ago,
  COALESCE(_s0.agg_0, 0) AS total_transactions,
  COALESCE(_s1.agg_1, 0) AS weekend_transactions
FROM _s0 AS _s0
LEFT JOIN _s1 AS _s1
  ON _s0.weeks_ago = _s1.weeks_ago
ORDER BY
  weeks_ago

# Define the time boundaries for the last 8 weeks, excluding the current week.
# 'start of week' truncates the current timestamp to the preceding Monday.
start_of_the_current_week = DATETIME('now', 'start of week')
# We then subtract 8 weeks to define the beginning of our analysis period.
start_of_the_lookback_period = DATETIME(start_of_the_current_week, '-8 weeks')

# 1. Filter transactions to our specific criteria:
#    - Must be for tickers of type 'stock'.
#    - Must fall within our defined 8-week time window.
# 2. For each of these transactions, calculate the start date of the week it belongs to.
#    This will be our key for grouping.
transactions_in_period = Transactions.WHERE(
    (LOWER(ticker.ticker_type) == 'stock') &
    (date_time >= start_of_the_lookback_period) &
    (date_time < start_of_the_current_week)
).CALCULATE(
    week_start=DATETIME(date_time, 'start of week')
)

# 3. Partition (group) the filtered transactions by their calculated week_start date.
# 4. For each weekly group, calculate the total number of transactions and the count of weekend transactions.
#    Weekend days are identified as 'Saturday' or 'Sunday'.
# 5. Order the results by week in descending order to show the most recent weeks first.
weekly_stock_transactions = transactions_in_period.PARTITION(name='weeks', by=week_start).CALCULATE(
    week_start_date=week_start,
    total_transactions=COUNT(Transactions),
    weekend_transactions=COUNT(Transactions.WHERE(ISIN(DAYNAME(date_time), ('Saturday', 'Sunday'))))
).ORDER_BY(week_start_date.DESC())

# Define the start of the current week and the start of the 8-week period.
# The week is configured to start on Monday.
start_of_this_week = DATETIME('now', 'start of week')
start_of_period = DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-8 weeks')

# Filter transactions to include only stocks within the last 8 full weeks.
recent_stock_transactions = Transactions.WHERE(
    (LOWER(ticker.ticker_type) == 'stock') &
    (date_time >= start_of_period) &
    (date_time < start_of_this_week)
)

# For each transaction, calculate its week's start date and check if it occurred on a weekend.
transactions_with_week_info = recent_stock_transactions.CALCULATE(
    week_start_date=DATETIME(date_time, 'start of week'),
    is_weekend=ISIN(DAYNAME(date_time), ("Saturday", "Sunday"))
)

# Group transactions by their week's start date and calculate the required counts.
weekly_transaction_counts = transactions_with_week_info.PARTITION(
    name="weekly_groups",
    by=(week_start_date)
).CALCULATE(
    week_start_date=week_start_date,
    total_transactions=COUNT(Transactions),
    weekend_transactions=SUM(Transactions.is_weekend)
).ORDER_BY(week_start_date.ASC())

result = weekly_transaction_counts

[DEBUG] [Q1] Code generated by claude:
# Get transactions from the last 8 weeks excluding the current week
# Current week starts from the most recent Monday
# We need transactions from 8 weeks before that
transactions_info = Transactions.WHERE(
    # Filter for transactions in the last 9 weeks (we'll exclude current week later)
    DATEDIFF("weeks", date_time, DATETIME("now")) <= 8
).CALCULATE(
    # Calculate the week number relative to now
    weeks_ago = DATEDIFF("weeks", date_time, DATETIME("now")),
    # Get the day of week (1=Monday, 7=Sunday based on the start_of_week config)
    day_of_week = DAYOFWEEK(date_time),
    # Check if it's a weekend (Saturday=6, Sunday=7)
    is_weekend = (DAYOFWEEK(date_time) == 6) | (DAYOFWEEK(date_time) == 7)
)

# Filter out the current week (weeks_ago = 0) and group by week
result = transactions_info.WHERE(
    weeks_ago > 0
).PARTITION(
    name="weekly_groups", 
    by=weeks_ago
).CALCULATE(
    weeks_ago = weeks_ago,
    total_transactions = COUNT(Transactions),
    weekend_transactions = SUM(Transactions.CALCULATE(is_weekend_int = IFF(is_weekend, 1, 0)).is_weekend_int)
).ORDER_BY(weeks_ago.ASC())

# Define the start of the current week (Monday) and the start of the previous week.
# 'start of week' defaults to Monday.
start_of_current_week = DATETIME('now', 'start of week')
start_of_last_week = DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-7 days')

# Filter transactions to include only those from customers in the USA that occurred last week.
last_week_usa_transactions = Transactions.WHERE(
    (LOWER(customer.country) == 'usa') &
    (date_time >= start_of_last_week) &
    (date_time < start_of_current_week)
)

    number_of_transactions=COUNT(last_week_usa_transactions),

# Calculate the start of the current week (Monday) and the date range for last week
# Current week starts at the most recent Monday
# Last week is from the Monday before that to the Sunday before the current Monday
result = Broker.CALCULATE(
    # Start of current week (Monday)
    current_week_start = DATETIME('now', 'start of week'),
    # Start of last week (Monday of previous week) 
    last_week_start = DATETIME(DATETIME('now', 'start of week'), '-7 days'),
    # End of last week (Sunday, which is 1 day before current week start)
    last_week_end = DATETIME(DATETIME('now', 'start of week'), '-1 day')
).Customers.WHERE(
    LOWER(country) == LOWER('USA')
).transactions_made.WHERE(
    (date_time >= last_week_start) & (date_time <= last_week_end)
).CALCULATE(
    transaction_count = COUNT(Transactions),
    total_amount = SUM(amount)
).SINGULAR()

    LOWER(country) == LOWER("USA")
).transactions_made.WHERE(
    # Start of last week: current date, start of week, minus 7 days
    (date_time >= DATETIME("now", "start of week", "-7 days")) &
    # End of last week: current date, start of week, minus 1 day
    (date_time < DATETIME("now", "start of week"))
)

# Calculate the count and total amount
result = Broker.CALCULATE(
    num_transactions=COUNT(usa_transactions_last_week),
    total_amount=SUM(usa_transactions_last_week.amount)
)

# Get customers who signed up in the last 6 months excluding current month
# First, calculate the date range
recent_customers = Customers.WHERE(
    (join_date >= DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-6 months')) &
    (join_date < DATETIME('now', 'start of month'))
).CALCULATE(
    signup_month = JOIN_STRINGS('-', YEAR(join_date), LPAD(MONTH(join_date), 2, '0'))
)

# Partition by signup month to calculate PMCS and PMAT
result = recent_customers.PARTITION(name='months', by=signup_month).CALCULATE(
    month = signup_month,
    PMCS = COUNT(Customers),
    PMAT = AVG(Customers.transactions_made.amount)
)

customer_signups_and_transactions = Broker.CALCULATE(

    start_of_period=DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-6 months')
).Customers.WHERE(

).CALCULATE(

).PARTITION(

).CALCULATE(
    month=signup_month,
    PMCS=COUNT(Customers),
    PMAT=AVG(Customers.transactions_made.amount)
).ORDER_BY(month.ASC())

# First, filter customers who joined in the last 6 months excluding current month
# We need to calculate the date range
customers_last_6_months = Customers.WHERE(
    (DATEDIFF("months", join_date, DATETIME("now")) <= 6) & 
    (DATEDIFF("months", join_date, DATETIME("now")) > 0)
).CALCULATE(
    join_month=JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(join_date), LPAD(MONTH(join_date), 2, "0")),
    customer_id=_id
)

# Partition by join month to calculate PMCS
monthly_signups = customers_last_6_months.PARTITION(
    name="months", 
    by=join_month
).CALCULATE(
    month=join_month,
    pmcs=COUNT(Customers),
    # For PMAT, we need to calculate average transaction amount for all transactions in that month
    pmat=AVG(Customers.transactions_made.amount)
)

result = monthly_signups.ORDER_BY(month.ASC())

# Get customers who signed up in the last 6 months excluding current month
# First, let's calculate the date range
recent_customers = Customers.WHERE(
    (DATEDIFF("months", join_date, DATETIME("now")) <= 6) & 
    (DATEDIFF("months", join_date, DATETIME("now")) >= 1)
).CALCULATE(
    signup_month = JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(join_date), LPAD(MONTH(join_date), 2, "0"))
)

# Partition by signup month to get PMCS and PMAT
result = recent_customers.PARTITION(name="months", by=signup_month).CALCULATE(
    month = signup_month,
    PMCS = COUNT(Customers),
    PMAT = AVG(Customers.transactions_made.amount)
).ORDER_BY(month.ASC())

).Customers.WHERE(

).CALCULATE(
    signup_month=JOIN_STRINGS('-', YEAR(join_date), LPAD(MONTH(join_date), 2, '0'))
).PARTITION(

).CALCULATE(
    month=signup_month,
    PMCS=COUNT(Customers),

# Filter sell transactions from the past month
sell_transactions_past_month = Transactions.WHERE(
    (LOWER(transaction_type) == 'sell') & 
    (DATEDIFF("days", date_time, DATETIME('now')) <= 30)
)

# Group by ticker symbol and calculate SPM
result = sell_transactions_past_month.PARTITION(
    name="ticker_groups", 
    by=(ticker.symbol,)
).CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol=ticker.symbol,
    total_amount=SUM(Transactions.amount),
    total_tax=SUM(Transactions.tax),
    total_commission=SUM(Transactions.commission)
).CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol=ticker_symbol,
    SPM=100.0 * (total_amount - (total_tax + total_commission)) / total_amount
)

[DEBUG] [Q4] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Filter transactions to 'sell' types within the last month and extract the ticker symbol for grouping.
sell_transactions_info = Transactions.WHERE(
    (LOWER(transaction_type) == 'sell') &
    (date_time >= DATETIME('now', '-1 month'))
).CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol=ticker.symbol
)

# Step 2: Partition the filtered transactions by ticker symbol.
# For each ticker group, calculate the sum of amount, tax, and commission.
ticker_groups = sell_transactions_info.PARTITION(
    name="ticker_groups",
    by=(ticker_symbol)
).CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol=ticker_symbol,
    total_amount_from_sells=SUM(Transactions.amount),
    total_tax=SUM(Transactions.tax),
    total_commission=SUM(Transactions.commission)
)

# Step 3: Calculate the final SPM using the aggregated values from the previous step.
# This includes a check to prevent division by zero if the total amount is zero.
# The final result includes only the ticker symbol and the calculated SPM.
result = ticker_groups.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol=ticker_symbol,
    spm=IFF(
        total_amount_from_sells > 0,
        (total_amount_from_sells - (total_tax + total_commission)) / total_amount_from_sells * 100.0,
        0.0
    )
)

# Step 1: Filter transactions to include only 'sell' types from the last month (inclusive).
# Also, extract the ticker symbol to be used as a partition key.
sell_transactions_last_month = Transactions.WHERE(
    (LOWER(transaction_type) == 'sell') &
    (date_time >= DATETIME('now', '-1 month'))
).CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol=ticker.symbol
)

# Step 2: Partition the filtered transactions by ticker symbol to group them.
ticker_groups = sell_transactions_last_month.PARTITION(name="tickers", by=(ticker_symbol))

# Step 3: For each ticker group, calculate the total sell amount and total costs (tax + commission).
# Then, use a subsequent CALCULATE to compute the SPM using the provided formula.
result = ticker_groups.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol=ticker_symbol,
    total_amount_from_sells=SUM(Transactions.amount),
    total_costs=SUM(Transactions.tax) + SUM(Transactions.commission)
).CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol=ticker_symbol,
    spm=IFF(
        total_amount_from_sells > 0,
        (total_amount_from_sells - total_costs) / total_amount_from_sells * 100.0,
        0.0
    )
)

# Get sales with sale price over $30,000 and their payments
high_value_sales = Sales.WHERE(sale_price > 30000)

# Get all payments for these high-value sales with week information
payments_with_week_info = high_value_sales.payment.CALCULATE(
    payment_date=payment_date,
    # Calculate the start of the week (Monday) for each payment
    week_start=DATETIME(payment_date, 'start of week'),
    # Determine if payment was on weekend (Saturday=6, Sunday=0 in some systems, but let's use DAYNAME)
    is_weekend=IFF(
        (DAYNAME(payment_date) == "Saturday") | (DAYNAME(payment_date) == "Sunday"),
        1,
        0
    )
)

# Filter to only include payments from the last 8 weeks (excluding current week)
# Current week starts at the most recent Monday
current_week_start = DATETIME('now', 'start of week')
eight_weeks_ago = DATETIME(current_week_start, '-56 days')  # 8 weeks * 7 days = 56 days

filtered_payments = payments_with_week_info.WHERE(
    (week_start >= eight_weeks_ago) & (week_start < current_week_start)
)

# Group by week and calculate totals
result = filtered_payments.PARTITION(name="weeks", by=week_start).CALCULATE(
    week=week_start,
    total_payments_received=COUNT(payment),
    weekend_payments_received=SUM(payment.is_weekend)
).ORDER_BY(week.ASC())

[DEBUG] [Q6] Code generated by gemini:
weekly_payment_summary = Dealership.CALCULATE(
    # Define the start of the current week, assuming weeks start on Monday.
    start_of_current_week=DATETIME('now', 'start of week'),
    # Define the start of the 8-week period we are interested in.
    start_of_period=DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-8 weeks')
).Sales.WHERE(
    # Filter for sales with a price over $30,000.
    sale_price > 30000
).payment.WHERE(
    # Filter payments to include only those within the last 8 weeks, excluding the current week.
    (payment_date >= start_of_period) & (payment_date < start_of_current_week)
).CALCULATE(
    # For each payment, determine the start of its week.
    week_start_date=DATETIME(payment_date, 'start of week'),
    # Flag payments that occurred on a weekend (Saturday or Sunday).
    is_weekend=ISIN(DAYNAME(payment_date), ("Saturday", "Sunday"))
).PARTITION(
    # Group the filtered payments by the start date of their week.
    name="weekly_groups", by=(week_start_date)
).CALCULATE(
    # For each weekly group, calculate the required metrics.
    week=week_start_date,
    total_payments_received=COUNT(payment),
    weekend_payments_received=SUM(is_weekend)
).ORDER_BY(
    # Sort the final results by week in ascending order.
    week.ASC()
)

# Get sales with sale price over $30,000 and their payments
high_value_sales_payments = Sales.WHERE(sale_price > 30000).payment.CALCULATE(
    payment_date=payment_date,
    # Calculate the week start date (Monday) for each payment
    week_start=DATETIME(payment_date, 'start of week'),
    # Determine if payment was on weekend (Saturday=6, Sunday=0 in week starting Monday)
    is_weekend=ISIN(DAYOFWEEK(payment_date), (6, 7))
)

# Filter to last 8 weeks excluding current week
# Current week start
current_week_start = DATETIME('now', 'start of week')
# 8 weeks ago from the start of current week
eight_weeks_ago = DATETIME(current_week_start, '-56 days')

filtered_payments = high_value_sales_payments.WHERE(
    (week_start >= eight_weeks_ago) & (week_start < current_week_start)
)

# Group by week and calculate counts
result = filtered_payments.PARTITION(name="weeks", by=week_start).CALCULATE(
    week=week_start,
    total_payments_received=COUNT(payment),
    weekend_payments_received=SUM(is_weekend)
).ORDER_BY(week.ASC())

[DEBUG] [Q6] Code generated by gemini:
# Define the start of the current week and the beginning of the 8-week analysis window.
# A week is considered to start on Monday as per the request.
start_of_current_week = DATETIME('now', 'start of week')
start_of_window = DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-8 weeks')

# Filter payments to include only those from sales over $30,000 and within the last 8 weeks.
# Also, calculate the week's start date and a flag for weekend payments.
payments_in_window = Dealership.PaymentsReceived.WHERE(
    (sale_record.sale_price > 30000) &
    (payment_date >= start_of_window) &
    (payment_date < start_of_current_week)
).CALCULATE(
    week_start=DATETIME(payment_date, 'start of week'),
    is_weekend=ISIN(DAYNAME(payment_date), ("Saturday", "Sunday"))
)

# Group the filtered payments by the start of the week.
# Then, for each week, count the total payments and the sum of weekend payments.
weekly_payment_summary = payments_in_window.PARTITION(
    name="weekly_groups", by=(week_start)
).CALCULATE(
    week=week_start,
    total_payments_received=COUNT(PaymentsReceived),
    weekend_payments_received=SUM(is_weekend)
).ORDER_BY(week.ASC())

result = weekly_payment_summary

# Get sales with sale price over $30,000 and their payments
high_value_sales_payments = Sales.WHERE(sale_price > 30000).payment.CALCULATE(
    payment_date=payment_date,
    # Calculate the week start date (Monday) for each payment
    week_start=DATETIME(payment_date, 'start of week'),
    # Determine if payment was on weekend (Saturday=6, Sunday=0 in DAYOFWEEK)
    is_weekend=ISIN(DAYOFWEEK(payment_date), (6, 0))
)

# Filter to only include payments from the last 8 weeks (excluding current week)
# Current week starts at DATETIME('now', 'start of week')
# 8 weeks ago starts at DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-8 weeks')
filtered_payments = high_value_sales_payments.WHERE(
    (week_start >= DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-8 weeks')) &
    (week_start < DATETIME('now', 'start of week'))
)

# Group by week and calculate counts
result = filtered_payments.PARTITION(name="weeks", by=week_start).CALCULATE(
    week=week_start,
    total_payments_received=COUNT(payment),
    weekend_payments_received=SUM(is_weekend)
).ORDER_BY(week.ASC())

[DEBUG] [Q6] Code generated by gemini:
# Define the time window for the last 8 weeks, excluding the current week.
# The week is defined as starting on Monday.
date_boundaries = Dealership.CALCULATE(
    start_of_current_week=DATETIME('now', 'start of week'),
    start_of_8_weeks_ago=DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-8 weeks')
)

# 1. Start with payments and filter them based on the sale price and date window.
# 2. Calculate the week's start date and a weekend flag for each relevant payment.
payments_info = date_boundaries.PaymentsReceived.WHERE(
    (sale_record.sale_price > 30000) &
    (payment_date >= start_of_8_weeks_ago) &
    (payment_date < start_of_current_week)
).CALCULATE(
    week_start_date=DATETIME(payment_date, 'start of week'),
    is_weekend=ISIN(DAYNAME(payment_date), ("Saturday", "Sunday"))
)

# 3. Partition the payments by week to group them.
# 4. For each week, calculate the total and weekend payment counts.
weekly_summary = payments_info.PARTITION(
    name="weekly_groups", by=(week_start_date)
).CALCULATE(
    week=week_start_date,
    total_payments_received=COUNT(PaymentsReceived),
    weekend_payments_received=SUM(PaymentsReceived.is_weekend)
)

# 5. Order the final result by week in ascending order.
result = weekly_summary.ORDER_BY(week.ASC())

# Define the start of the current week and the start of the 8-week analysis period.
start_of_current_week = DATETIME('now', 'start of week')
start_of_period = DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-8 weeks')

# Filter sales over $30,000, access their payments, and filter for the last 8 weeks.
# Also, calculate the week start date and a weekend flag for each payment.
payments_in_window = Sales.WHERE(sale_price > 30000).payment.WHERE(
    (payment_date >= start_of_period) & (payment_date < start_of_current_week)
).CALCULATE(
    week_start_date=DATETIME(payment_date, 'start of week'),
    is_weekend=IFF(DAYOFWEEK(payment_date) >= 6, 1, 0)
)

    name="weekly_groups", by=(week_start_date,)
).CALCULATE(
    week=week_start_date,
    total_payments_received=COUNT(PaymentsReceived),
    weekend_payments_received=SUM(PaymentsReceived.is_weekend)
).ORDER_BY(week.ASC())

    )
)

# Filter to only include payments from the last 8 weeks (excluding current week)
# Current week starts at the most recent Monday
current_week_start = DATETIME('now', 'start of week')
eight_weeks_ago = DATETIME(current_week_start, '-56 days')  # 8 weeks * 7 days = 56 days

filtered_payments = payments_with_week_info.WHERE(
    (week_start >= eight_weeks_ago) & (week_start < current_week_start)
)

# Group by week and calculate totals
result = filtered_payments.PARTITION(name="weeks", by=week_start).CALCULATE(
    week=week_start,
    total_payments_received=COUNT(payment),
    weekend_payments_received=SUM(payment.is_weekend)
).ORDER_BY(week.ASC())

# Define the start of the current week and the beginning of the 8-week analysis window.
# A week is considered to start on Monday as per the request.
start_of_current_week = DATETIME('now', 'start of week')
start_of_window = DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-8 weeks')

# Filter payments to include only those from sales over $30,000 and within the last 8 weeks.
# Also, calculate the week's start date and a flag for weekend payments.
payments_in_window = Dealership.PaymentsReceived.WHERE(
    (sale_record.sale_price > 30000) &
    (payment_date >= start_of_window) &
    (payment_date < start_of_current_week)
).CALCULATE(
    week_start=DATETIME(payment_date, 'start of week'),
    is_weekend=ISIN(DAYNAME(payment_date), ("Saturday", "Sunday"))
)

# Group the filtered payments by the start of the week.
# Then, for each week, count the total payments and the sum of weekend payments.
weekly_payment_summary = payments_in_window.PARTITION(
    name="weekly_groups", by=(week_start)
).CALCULATE(
    week=week_start,
    total_payments_received=COUNT(PaymentsReceived),
    weekend_payments_received=SUM(is_weekend)
).ORDER_BY(week.ASC())

result = weekly_payment_summary

# Define the date boundaries for the last 6 months, excluding the current month.
date_boundaries = Dealership.CALCULATE(
    start_date=DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-6 months'),
    end_date=DATETIME('now', 'start of month')
)

# Filter sales to the specified date range and for salespersons hired in 2022-2023.
# Also, create a 'sale_month' column for grouping.
sales_to_group = date_boundaries.Sales.WHERE(
    (sale_date >= start_date) &
    (sale_date < end_date) &
    (YEAR(salesperson.hire_date) >= 2022) &
    (YEAR(salesperson.hire_date) <= 2023)
).CALCULATE(
    sale_month=DATETIME(sale_date, 'start of month')
)

# Partition the filtered sales by month to calculate the required metrics.
# The result is ordered by month in ascending order.
monthly_summary = sales_to_group.PARTITION(
    name="sales_by_month",
    by=(sale_month)
).CALCULATE(
    month=sale_month,
    PMSPS=COUNT(Sales),
    PMSR=SUM(Sales.sale_price)
).ORDER_BY(month.ASC())

[DEBUG] [Q8] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Create a 'sale_month' column by truncating the 'sale_date' for each sale.
sales_with_month_info = Sales.CALCULATE(
    sale_month=DATETIME(sale_date, 'start of month')
)

# Step 2: Partition all sales by 'sale_month'. This creates a collection where each item represents a month,
# acting as a calendar based on sales data. This assumes at least one sale occurred in each of the last 6 months.
monthly_sales_groups = sales_with_month_info.PARTITION(name="sales_by_month", by=(sale_month))

# Step 3: Filter the monthly groups to the last 6 months (excluding the current month) and calculate metrics.
result = monthly_sales_groups.WHERE(
    (sale_month >= DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-6 months')) &
    (sale_month < DATETIME('now', 'start of month'))
).CALCULATE(
    month=sale_month,
    # For each month, create a temporary collection of sales from eligible salespersons.
    eligible_sales_in_month=Sales.WHERE(
        (YEAR(salesperson.hire_date) >= 2022) & (YEAR(salesperson.hire_date) <= 2023)
    ),
    # Calculate PMSPS (Per Month Salesperson Sales count) on the filtered sub-collection.
    PMSPS=COUNT(eligible_sales_in_month),
    # Calculate PMSR (Per Month Sales Revenue) on the filtered sub-collection, defaulting to 0 if no sales.
    PMSR=DEFAULT_TO(SUM(eligible_sales_in_month.sale_price), 0)
).CALCULATE(
    # Select the final columns for the output.
    month,
    PMSPS,
    PMSR
).ORDER_BY(month.ASC())

# Get the date range for the last 6 months excluding current month
current_month_start = DATETIME('now', 'start of month')
six_months_ago = DATETIME(current_month_start, '-6 months')

# Filter salespersons hired between 2022 and 2023
eligible_salespersons = Salespersons.WHERE(
    (YEAR(hire_date) >= 2022) & (YEAR(hire_date) <= 2023)
)

# Get all sales from eligible salespersons in the date range
sales_in_range = eligible_salespersons.sales_made.WHERE(
    (sale_date >= six_months_ago) & (sale_date < current_month_start)
).CALCULATE(
    salesperson_id=salesperson._id,
    salesperson_name=JOIN_STRINGS(' ', salesperson.first_name, salesperson.last_name),
    sale_month=DATETIME(sale_date, 'start of month'),
    sale_price=sale_price
)

# Partition by salesperson and month
monthly_groups = sales_in_range.PARTITION(
    name="sp_month_groups",
    by=(salesperson_id, salesperson_name, sale_month)
).CALCULATE(
    salesperson_id=salesperson_id,
    salesperson_name=salesperson_name,
    month=sale_month,
    pmsps=COUNT(sales_made),
    pmsr=SUM(sales_made.sale_price)
)

# Order by month
result = monthly_groups.ORDER_BY(month.ASC())

[DEBUG] [Q8] Code generated by gemini:
# Define the date range for the last 6 months, excluding the current month.
start_date = DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-6 months')
end_date = DATETIME('now', 'start of month')

# First, create a "scaffold" of all months in the last 6 months that have at least one sale.
# This is done by taking all sales, calculating their month, filtering for the date range,
# and then partitioning by month. This approach ensures that months with zero sales
# from the target group but non-zero sales from other groups are included.
sales_with_month = Sales.CALCULATE(
    sale_month=DATETIME(sale_date, 'start of month')
)

recent_months_scaffold = sales_with_month.WHERE(
    (sale_month >= start_date) & (sale_month < end_date)
).PARTITION(
    name="monthly_groups",
    by=(sale_month)
)

# Now, for each month in our scaffold, calculate the required metrics.
# The metrics are calculated by filtering the 'Sales' sub-collection within each partition
# to only include sales from salespersons hired between 2022 and 2023.
sales_metrics = recent_months_scaffold.CALCULATE(
    month=sale_month,
    # PMSPS: Count sales from the target salespersons for the given month.
    PMSPS=COUNT(
        Sales.WHERE(
            (YEAR(salesperson.hire_date) >= 2022) & (YEAR(salesperson.hire_date) <= 2023)
        )
    ),
    # PMSR: Sum the sale_price for the same filtered set of sales.
    # Use DEFAULT_TO to ensure months with no relevant sales show 0.0 instead of NULL.
    PMSR=DEFAULT_TO(
        SUM(
            Sales.WHERE(
                (YEAR(salesperson.hire_date) >= 2022) & (YEAR(salesperson.hire_date) <= 2023)
            ).sale_price
        ),
        0.0
    )
).ORDER_BY(month.ASC())

# The final result is assigned to the variable 'result'.
result = sales_metrics

# Define the date boundaries for the last 6 months, excluding the current month.
date_boundaries = Dealership.CALCULATE(
    start_date=DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-6 months'),
    end_date=DATETIME('now', 'start of month')
)

# Filter sales to the specified date range and for salespersons hired in 2022-2023.
# Also, create a 'sale_month' column for grouping.
sales_to_group = date_boundaries.Sales.WHERE(
    (sale_date >= start_date) &
    (sale_date < end_date) &
    (YEAR(salesperson.hire_date) >= 2022) &
    (YEAR(salesperson.hire_date) <= 2023)
).CALCULATE(
    sale_month=DATETIME(sale_date, 'start of month')
)

# Partition the filtered sales by month to calculate the required metrics.
# The result is ordered by month in ascending order.
monthly_summary = sales_to_group.PARTITION(
    name="sales_by_month",
    by=(sale_month)
).CALCULATE(
    month=sale_month,
    PMSPS=COUNT(Sales),
    PMSR=SUM(Sales.sale_price)
).ORDER_BY(month.ASC())

).CALCULATE(

# First, for each sale that has associated payments, we calculate the average number of days
# from the sale date to the payment date. This is done by calculating the day difference
# for each payment and then averaging those differences per sale.
per_sale_avg_days = Sales.WHERE(HAS(payment) == 1).CALCULATE(
    avg_days_to_pay_for_sale=AVG(
        payment.CALCULATE(
            day_diff=DATEDIFF("days", sale_date, payment_date)
        ).day_diff
    )
)

# Next, we calculate the overall average of these per-sale averages.
# This gives a single value representing the average payment time across all sales,
# which is then rounded to two decimal places as requested.
average_payment_time = Dealership.CALCULATE(
    average_days_to_payment=ROUND(AVG(per_sale_avg_days.avg_days_to_pay_for_sale), 2)
)

[DEBUG] [Q12] Code generated by claude:
# Get payments from the previous ISO week
previous_week_payments = PaymentsReceived.WHERE(
    # Payment date is in the previous week (7-13 days ago from the start of current week)
    (payment_date >= DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-7 days')) &
    (payment_date < DATETIME('now', 'start of week'))
)

# Group by payment date and payment method
result = previous_week_payments.PARTITION(
    name="daily_method_groups", 
    by=(payment_date, payment_method)
).CALCULATE(
    payment_date=payment_date,
    payment_method=payment_method,
    daily_total=SUM(PaymentsReceived.payment_amount)
).ORDER_BY(payment_date.ASC(), payment_method.ASC())
[DEBUG] [Q12] Executing code for claude
[DEBUG] [Q12] DataFrame from claude is valid

[DEBUG] [Q12] Code generated by gemini:
# Define the start and end dates for the previous week, assuming the week starts on Monday.
# 'start of week' gives the most recent Monday.
# '-7 days' from that gives the Monday of the previous week.
# '-1 day' from the start of the current week gives the Sunday of the previous week.
date_boundaries = Dealership.CALCULATE(
    start_of_previous_week=DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-7 days'),
    end_of_previous_week=DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-1 day')
)

# Filter the PaymentsReceived collection to include only records within the calculated date range.
payments_in_last_week = date_boundaries.PaymentsReceived.WHERE(
    (payment_date >= start_of_previous_week) & (payment_date <= end_of_previous_week)
)

# Partition the filtered payments by their date and payment method.
# Then, for each group, calculate the sum of the payment amounts.
daily_payment_summary = payments_in_last_week.PARTITION(
    name="daily_payments_by_method",
    by=(payment_date, payment_method)
).CALCULATE(
    payment_day=payment_date,
    method=payment_method,
    total_amount_received=SUM(PaymentsReceived.payment_amount)
)

# The previous week is the 7-day period before the start of the current week.

    start_of_current_week=DATETIME('now', 'start of week'),
    start_of_previous_week=DATETIME(DATETIME('now', 'start of week'), '-7 days')
)

# Filter payments to include only those from the previous week.
payments_in_previous_week = date_info.PaymentsReceived.WHERE(
    (payment_date >= start_of_previous_week) & (payment_date < start_of_current_week)
)

# Partition the filtered payments by date and method, then calculate the sum for each group.

    name="daily_method_groups",
    by=(payment_date, payment_method)
).CALCULATE(
    payment_day=payment_date,
    method=payment_method,
    total_received=SUM(PaymentsReceived.payment_amount)

# Define the start and end dates for the previous week, assuming the week starts on Monday.
# 'start of week' truncates the current date to the current week's Monday.
# '-7 days' goes back to the previous Monday.
# '-1 day' from the start of the current week gives the previous Sunday.
date_info = Dealership.CALCULATE(
    start_of_prev_week=DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-7 days'),
    end_of_prev_week=DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-1 day')
)

# Filter payments to include only those from the previous week.
payments_in_range = date_info.PaymentsReceived.WHERE(
    (payment_date >= start_of_prev_week) & (payment_date <= end_of_prev_week)
)

# Group the filtered payments by their date and payment method, then sum the amounts for each group.
daily_payment_summary = payments_in_range.PARTITION(
    name="daily_method_groups",
    by=(payment_date, payment_method)
).CALCULATE(
    payment_day=payment_date,
    method=payment_method,
    total_amount=SUM(PaymentsReceived.payment_amount)
).ORDER_BY(payment_day.ASC(), method.ASC())

).CALCULATE(

    patient_id=patient_id,
    diag_id=diag_id

[DEBUG] [Q14] Code generated by claude:
# Step 1: Get patients with treatments and find their first treatment year
patients_with_first_treatment = Patients.WHERE(
    HAS(treatments_received) == 1
).CALCULATE(
    patient_id,
    first_treatment_year = YEAR(MIN(treatments_received.start_dt))
)

# Step 2: Group by year to count new patients per year
new_patients_by_year = patients_with_first_treatment.PARTITION(
    name="years", 
    by=first_treatment_year
).CALCULATE(
    year = first_treatment_year,
    number_of_new_patients = COUNT(Patients)
)

# Step 3: Calculate NPI (increase from previous year) using window functions
result = new_patients_by_year.CALCULATE(
    year,
    number_of_new_patients,
    npi = number_of_new_patients - PREV(
        number_of_new_patients, 
        by=year.ASC(), 
        default=0
    )
).ORDER_BY(year.ASC())

# Step 1: For each patient with treatments, find the year of their first treatment.
patient_first_treatment_info = DermTreatment.Patients.WHERE(
    HAS(treatments_received) == 1
).CALCULATE(
    first_treatment_year=YEAR(MIN(treatments_received.start_dt))
)

# Step 2: Group patients by their first treatment year, count them, and order by year.
new_patients_per_year = patient_first_treatment_info.PARTITION(
    name="yearly_groups", by=first_treatment_year
).CALCULATE(
    year=first_treatment_year,
    num_new_patients=COUNT(Patients)
).ORDER_BY(year.ASC())

# Step 3: Calculate the year-over-year increase (NPINPI) using the PREV function.
npi_by_year = new_patients_per_year.CALCULATE(
    year=year,
    number_of_new_patients=num_new_patients,
    NPINPI=num_new_patients - PREV(num_new_patients, by=year.ASC())
)

# Step 1: For each patient with at least one treatment, determine the year of their first treatment.
new_patient_first_treatment_year = DermTreatment.Patients.WHERE(
    HAS(treatments_received) == 1
).CALCULATE(
    first_treatment_year=YEAR(MIN(treatments_received.start_dt))
)

# Step 2: Group patients by their first treatment year and count them to get the number of new patients per year.
new_patients_per_year = new_patient_first_treatment_year.PARTITION(
    name="yearly_cohorts", by=first_treatment_year
).CALCULATE(
    year=first_treatment_year,
    number_of_new_patients=COUNT(Patients)
)

# Step 3: Calculate the year-over-year increase (NPI) and sort the final result by year.
npi_by_year = new_patients_per_year.CALCULATE(
    year=year,
    number_of_new_patients=number_of_new_patients,
    NPI=number_of_new_patients - PREV(number_of_new_patients, by=year.ASC())
).ORDER_BY(year.ASC())

# Step 1: For each patient with treatments, find the year of their first treatment.
patient_first_treatment_year = DermTreatment.Patients.WHERE(
    HAS(treatments_received) == 1
).CALCULATE(
    first_treatment_year=YEAR(MIN(treatments_received.start_dt))
)

# Step 2: Group patients by their first treatment year and count them.
yearly_patient_counts = patient_first_treatment_year.PARTITION(
    name="year_groups", by=first_treatment_year
).CALCULATE(
    year=first_treatment_year,
    number_of_new_patients=COUNT(Patients)
)

# Step 3: Order by year and calculate the year-over-year increase (NPINPI).
npi_by_year = yearly_patient_counts.ORDER_BY(year.ASC()).CALCULATE(
    year=year,
    number_of_new_patients=number_of_new_patients,
    NPINPI=number_of_new_patients - PREV(number_of_new_patients, by=year.ASC())
)

# First, get patients with their first treatment year
patients_with_first_treatment = Patients.WHERE(
    HAS(treatments_received) == 1
).CALCULATE(
    patient_id,
    first_treatment_year = YEAR(
        treatments_received.WHERE(
            RANKING(by=start_dt.ASC(), per="Patients") == 1
        ).SINGULAR().start_dt
    )
)

# Group by year and count new patients
yearly_new_patients = patients_with_first_treatment.PARTITION(
    name="years", 
    by=first_treatment_year
).CALCULATE(
    year = first_treatment_year,
    num_new_patients = COUNT(Patients)
)

# Calculate NPI (increase from previous year)
result = yearly_new_patients.CALCULATE(
    year,
    num_new_patients,
    npi = num_new_patients - PREV(num_new_patients, by=year.ASC(), default=0)
).ORDER_BY(year.ASC())

merchant_cpur_stats = Merchants.CALCULATE(
    merchant_name=name,
    distinct_coupons_used=NDISTINCT(transactions_received.WHERE(LOWER(status) == 'success').coupon_id),
    distinct_successful_transactions=COUNT(transactions_received.WHERE(LOWER(status) == 'success'))
)
result = merchant_cpur_stats.CALCULATE(
    merchant_name=merchant_name,
    CPUR=IFF(
        distinct_successful_transactions > 0,
        distinct_coupons_used / distinct_successful_transactions,
        0.0
    )
)

[DEBUG] [Q16] Code generated by gemini:
merchant_cpur = Ewallet.Merchants.CALCULATE(
    merchant_name=name,
    # First, we isolate the successful transactions for each merchant.
    # A transaction is successful if its status is 'success'.
    successful_transactions=transactions_received.WHERE(LOWER(status) == 'success')
).CALCULATE(
    merchant_name=merchant_name,
    # Now, we calculate the CPUR using the successful transactions.
    # We use IFF to handle cases where a merchant has no successful transactions, preventing division by zero.
    CPUR=IFF(
        NDISTINCT(successful_transactions.txid) > 0,
        NDISTINCT(successful_transactions.coupon_id) / NDISTINCT(successful_transactions.txid),
        0.0
    )
)

# Define a contextless expression to filter for wallet transactions made by US users in the last 7 days.
# The date filter DATEDIFF("days", created_at, DATETIME('now')) <= 6 correctly captures the last 7 days, including today.
transactions_in_scope = Ewallet.Transactions.WHERE(
    (LOWER(transaction_type) == 'wallet') &
    (LOWER(sending_user.country) == 'us') &
    (DATEDIFF("days", created_at, DATETIME('now')) <= 6)
)

# Calculate the total count and sum of the amount for the filtered transactions.
wallet_transaction_summary = Ewallet.CALCULATE(
    num_transactions=COUNT(transactions_in_scope),
    total_transaction_amount=SUM(transactions_in_scope.amount)
)

# First, we filter the 'Transactions' collection to isolate the relevant transactions.
# These are transactions where the sender is from the 'US', the transaction type is 'WALLET',
# and the transaction occurred within the last 7 days (inclusive of today).
recent_us_wallet_transactions = Ewallet.Transactions.WHERE(
    (LOWER(sending_user.country) == 'us') &
    (LOWER(transaction_type) == 'wallet') &
    (DATEDIFF("days", created_at, DATETIME('now')) < 7)
)

# Then, we use the filtered collection to calculate the final aggregate metrics.
# We count the number of transactions and sum their amounts.
result = Ewallet.CALCULATE(
    number_of_transactions=COUNT(recent_us_wallet_transactions),
    total_transaction_amount=SUM(recent_us_wallet_transactions.amount)
)

# Get transactions from the last 2 months excluding current month
# First, calculate the date range
two_months_ago = DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-2 months')
current_month_start = DATETIME('now', 'start of month')

# Filter transactions and calculate month
transaction_info = Transactions.WHERE(
    (sender_type == 0) & 
    (created_at >= two_months_ago) & 
    (created_at < current_month_start)
).CALCULATE(
    sender_id=sender_id,
    month=JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(created_at), LPAD(MONTH(created_at), 2, "0"))
)

# Partition by month and count distinct senders
result = transaction_info.PARTITION(
    name="monthly_groups", 
    by=month
).CALCULATE(
    month=month,
    pmdau=NDISTINCT(Transactions.sender_id)
).ORDER_BY(month.ASC())

[DEBUG] [Q20] Code generated by gemini:
# Define the time boundaries for the last 2 full months, excluding the current month.
start_of_current_month = DATETIME('now', 'start of month')
start_of_period = DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-2 months')

# Filter transactions for sender_type=0 and within the specified time range,
# and extract date components for daily aggregation.
filtered_transactions = Ewallet.Transactions.WHERE(
    (sender_type == 0) &
    (created_at >= start_of_period) &
    (created_at < start_of_current_month)
).CALCULATE(
    transaction_year=YEAR(created_at),
    transaction_month=MONTH(created_at),
    transaction_day=DAY(created_at),
    sender_id=sender_id
)

# Partition by day to calculate the Daily Active Users (DAU) by counting distinct senders.
daily_active_users = filtered_transactions.PARTITION(
    name="daily_groups",
    by=(transaction_year, transaction_month, transaction_day)
).CALCULATE(
    transaction_year=transaction_year,
    transaction_month=transaction_month,
    dau=NDISTINCT(Transactions.sender_id)
)

# Partition the daily stats by month to calculate the Per Month Daily Active Users (PMDAU)
# by averaging the daily counts.
result = daily_active_users.PARTITION(
    name="monthly_groups",
    by=(transaction_year, transaction_month)
).CALCULATE(
    month=JOIN_STRINGS('-', transaction_year, LPAD(transaction_month, 2, '0')),
    pmdau=AVG(daily_active_users.dau)
)

    (sender_type == 0) &

    (DATEDIFF("months", created_at, DATETIME("now")) <= 2)
).CALCULATE(

[DEBUG] [Q20] Code generated by gemini:
# Define the time window: the start of two months ago to the start of the current month.
start_of_period = DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-2 months')
end_of_period = DATETIME('now', 'start of month')

# Filter transactions to the relevant time window and user type.
# Also, pre-calculate date components (year, month, day) for partitioning.
transactions_in_period = Ewallet.Transactions.WHERE(
    (sender_type == 0) &
    (created_at >= start_of_period) &
    (created_at < end_of_period)
).CALCULATE(
    year=YEAR(created_at),
    month=MONTH(created_at),
    day=DAY(created_at),
    sender_id=sender_id
)

# First, partition by day to calculate the Daily Active Users (DAU).
# DAU is the number of distinct senders for each day.
daily_active_users = transactions_in_period.PARTITION(
    name="daily_groups",
    by=(year, month, day)
).CALCULATE(
    year=year,
    month=month,
    dau_count=NDISTINCT(Transactions.sender_id)
)

# Next, partition the daily results by month to calculate the average DAU for each month (PMDAU).
pmdau_by_month = daily_active_users.PARTITION(
    name="monthly_groups",
    by=(year, month)
).CALCULATE(
    # Format the month string as 'yyyy-mm'.
    month_formatted=JOIN_STRINGS('-', year, LPAD(month, 2, '0')),
    # Calculate PMDAU by averaging the daily counts within the month.
    pmdau=AVG(daily_groups.dau_count)
)

# Select the final fields for the output.
pmdau_result = pmdau_by_month.CALCULATE(
    month=month_formatted,
    pmdau=pmdau
)

[DEBUG] [Q20] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Define the time boundaries for the last two full months.
time_boundaries = Ewallet.CALCULATE(
    start_of_current_month=DATETIME('now', 'start of month'),
    start_of_period=DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-2 months')
)

# Step 2: Filter transactions to the specified period and sender type, and extract date components.
transactions_in_period = time_boundaries.Transactions.WHERE(
    (sender_type == 0) &
    (created_at >= start_of_period) &
    (created_at < start_of_current_month)
).CALCULATE(
    transaction_year=YEAR(created_at),
    transaction_month=MONTH(created_at),
    transaction_day=DAY(created_at)
)

# Step 3: Group by day to calculate the Daily Active Users (DAU).
daily_active_user_counts = transactions_in_period.PARTITION(
    name="daily_groups",
    by=(transaction_year, transaction_month, transaction_day)
).CALCULATE(
    transaction_year,
    transaction_month,
    dau=NDISTINCT(Transactions.sender_id)
)

# Step 4: Group the daily counts by month to calculate the average DAU (PMDAU) for each month.
# The month is formatted as 'yyyy-mm'.
pmdau_by_month = daily_active_user_counts.CALCULATE(
    month_yyyy_mm=JOIN_STRINGS('-', transaction_year, LPAD(transaction_month, 2, '0'))
).PARTITION(
    name="monthly_groups",
    by=(month_yyyy_mm)
).CALCULATE(
    month=month_yyyy_mm,
    pmdau=AVG(daily_active_user_counts.dau)
)

result = pmdau_by_month

result = Users.CALCULATE(
    user_id=uid,
    total_duration=SUM(
        sessions.WHERE(
            (session_start_ts >= "2023-06-01") & 
            (session_start_ts < "2023-06-08")
        ).CALCULATE(
            duration_seconds=DATEDIFF("seconds", session_start_ts, session_end_ts)
        ).duration_seconds
    )
).WHERE(
    HAS(sessions.WHERE(
        (session_start_ts >= "2023-06-01") & 
        (session_start_ts < "2023-06-08")
    )) == 1
).ORDER_BY(total_duration.DESC())
